## Status Meanings

- `Proved`
  - the surviving behavior is backed by cited Go implementation and cited Go
    proof.
- `Implemented, proof incomplete`
  - Go code appears to implement the behavior, but the current proof does not
    yet match the source-baseline evidence closely enough.
- `Missing`
  - the source-baseline behavior does not currently have an equivalent Go
    behavior.
- `Retained Python boundary`
  - the behavior intentionally remains on an explicit retained Python
    authoring/client surface rather than being a Go port target.
- `Dormant / unproven caller`
  - the source-baseline behavior exists, but this matrix slice does not yet
    prove that a direct Go equivalent is still required by active callers or
    by the current Go architecture.
- `Intentional divergence`
  - the source-baseline behavior changed on purpose, and the rewrite docs or
    active-caller contract explicitly document the clean break.

- Forty-four concrete parity gaps are already visible:
  - source-baseline prompt-template updates refresh linked prompt-section
    cached state
  - current Go does not run an equivalent linked-section refresh
  - source-baseline prompt-template delete blocks workflow-edge references
    with `409`
  - current Go deletes referenced workflow templates without that guard
  - source-baseline listener registration changes push
    `ListenerCoverageSnapshot` updates over the frontend websocket
  - current Go only exposes the pull route and does not emit the websocket
    update
  - source-baseline listener transport supports `use_temporary_ids`
  - current Go rejects temporary listener ids on `ListenerInit`
  - source-baseline share-scope UI-setting reveal routing is constrained to the
    active `shareSessionId`
  - current Go reveal routing ignores share-session scope and treats frontend
    selection globally
  - source-baseline reveal-path routing returns the same allowlist `400` for
    missing paths outside approved roots
  - current Go reveals leak `404 path_not_found` before the allowlist check on
    those missing outside-root paths
  - source-baseline managed and external chat hydration responses preserve
    `appGenerationByMessageId` provenance from run inputs
  - current Go hydration responses omit that field even though current
    frontend hydration still consumes it
  - source-baseline `getAIFollowSnapshot` merges managed and external
    AI-follow state for the active project
  - current Go only returns external-agent follow state from that route
  - source-baseline workflow chats with signal-filtered outgoing edges fail
    closed when the selected agent profile cannot call
    `report_state_machine_signal`
  - current Go workflow startup does not validate that precondition even
    though current docs still describe the fail-closed behavior
  - source-baseline stateless `/__setta__/tools/call` agentic context carries
    `runId` for `report_state_machine_signal`
  - current Go drops `runId` from that request-body transport, so direct HTTP
    signal-tool calls cannot satisfy the source contract
  - source-baseline `getAgentProfiles` / `getAgentProfile` remain available
    anywhere LLM access is allowed, including share/deployment reads
  - current Go hard-gates every agent-profile route to IDE mode even though
    current share/deployment UI still hydrates profile reads
  - source-baseline `setupLauncher` rejects unknown extra JSON fields with
    FastAPI/Pydantic `422` validation
  - current Go launcher setup strict-decode path returns `400 invalid_json`
    for the same unknown-field payloads
  - source-baseline `setChartAreaDataPath` persists the literal `dataPath`
    string provided by the caller
  - current Go trims leading and trailing whitespace before persisting that
    chart data path
  - source-baseline invalid `setChartType` / `setChartSettings` request bodies
    fail FastAPI/Pydantic validation with `422`
  - current Go normalizes chart-type payloads after JSON decode and returns
    `400` chart-mutation errors instead
  - source-baseline invalid browser `viewMode` / saved-search `searchScope`
    payloads fail FastAPI/Pydantic validation with `422`
  - current Go trims those values, returns `400` browser-mutation errors, and
    also coerces blank `searchScope` strings to `recursive`
  - source-baseline browser metadata mutation routes stay mounted in
    share/app sessions under `allowLayoutChanges`
  - current Go mounts those routes only in IDE even though the current browser
    UI still wires view-mode, saved-search, hide-column, and width mutations
    through the shared layout surface
  - source-baseline invalid spreadsheet `density` and task-board `viewMode`
    request bodies fail FastAPI/Pydantic validation with `422`
  - current Go trims those enum-like values after decode and returns `400`
    section-data mutation errors instead
  - source-baseline spreadsheet and task-board mutation routes stay mounted in
    share/app sessions under `allowLayoutChanges`
  - current Go mounts those routes only in IDE even though the current
    spreadsheet and task-board surfaces still wire them through
    `canEditLayout`
  - source-baseline invalid chat-section `permissionMode` / `dictationMode`
    request bodies fail FastAPI/Pydantic validation with `422`
  - current Go trims those enum-like values after decode and returns `400`
    chat-section mutation errors instead
  - source non-IDE share/app sessions keep chat-section settings/state routes
    mounted behind `allowLayoutChanges` and chat-thread binding writes behind
    `allowLLM`
  - current Go mounts that direct HTTP chat-section route family only in IDE
    even though the current chat surfaces still persist state/settings and
    managed-thread resets through share-aware callers
  - source-baseline plugin install rejects removed legacy scope
    `environment` with FastAPI/Pydantic `422` validation
  - current Go plugin install maps that payload to `400 PLUGIN_SCOPE_INVALID`
  - source-baseline plugin routes preserve manager-provided HTTP status and
    structured code/message payloads for runtime/rescan and mutation failures
  - current Go plugin route error mapping only preserves a fixed subset of
    service codes and otherwise falls back to `400` or `500 internal_error`
  - source-baseline unauthorized non-API HTTP routes return the bootstrap HTML
    page instead of a JSON payload
  - current Go returns the structured JSON error envelope for every auth
    failure, including authenticated root routes like `/mcp`
  - source-baseline dev-mode localhost requests from the allowed Vite
    Origin/Referer auto-mint an auth cookie
  - current Go only reflects CORS headers for that origin and still returns
    `401 unauthorized`
  - source-baseline near-expiry auth cookies refresh only on `/api/*`
    responses
  - current Go refreshes cookies on every authenticated route, including root
    transports such as `/mcp`
  - source-baseline auth-cookie refresh also extends trusted frontend-origin
    expiry for the current session
  - current Go refreshes the cookie without updating the trusted-origin store
  - source publish and deployment project-config request models reject unknown
    extra JSON fields
  - current Go deployment publish/project-config handlers use permissive JSON
    decoding and silently accept those extra fields
  - source deployment sessions auto-start enabled `listener/app.py`
    entrypoints on deployment websocket/state bootstrap
  - current Go publishes listener metadata and still documents that behavior,
    but it never launches a retained loader or equivalent per deployment
    session
  - source code-section diagnostics ignore versionless LSP publishes
  - current Go applies versionless code-section diagnostics directly into the
    live snapshot instead of dropping them
  - source invalid text-block display-mode / format request bodies fail typed
    FastAPI enum validation with `422`
  - current Go trims those enum strings and returns `400` from the mutation
    path instead
  - source deployment sessions keep text-block edit/layout mutation routes
    mounted behind `allowParamChanges` / `allowLayoutChanges`
  - current Go hard-gates all text-block mutation routes to IDE mode even
    though current deployment UI still wires those controls through
    `useShareCapabilities`
  - source share/app sessions keep `moveSections` mounted behind
    `allowLayoutChanges`
  - current Go mounts `moveSections` only in IDE even though current shared
    layout drag still routes ordinary moves through `route_move_sections`
  - source share/app sessions keep `setCodeSectionAttachedTerminal`
    transport-visible and fail closed with deployment-mode structural `403`
  - current Go mounts that special-case attached-terminal route only in IDE
    even though current shared DnD still switches to it for terminal drops
  - source share/app sessions keep `createChildSections` transport-visible and
    fail closed with deployment-mode structural `403`
  - current Go mounts `createChildSections` only in IDE even though the
    current non-user code-section header still offers attached-terminal
    creation through that route
  - source share/app sessions keep `deleteSections` transport-visible and fail
    closed with deployment-mode structural `403`
  - current Go mounts `deleteSections` only in IDE even though the current
    plugin fallback UI still offers direct section deletion outside user-view
    mode

## Deployment Listener Manifest And Runtime

| ID | Behavior | Source-baseline evidence (`main`) | Go implementation / proof | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `DL-01` | Publishing share/app deployments preserves listener config, rewrites the listener entrypoint into the canonical `listener/app.py` deployment path, copies the listener file into the deployment root, and defaults `listener.enabled` to `true` when omitted from the deployment manifest. | Source docs: `docs/reference/sharing-mode-vs-ide-mode.md`; source model: `backend/setta/types/share_deployment.py::{DeploymentListenerConfig,DeploymentManifest}`; source retained runtime: `backend/setta/share/listener_runtime.py`. | Impl: `internal/deployments/{share_publish.go,app_publish.go,manifest.go}`; proof: `internal/deployments/share_publish_test.go::TestSharePublisherCopiesListenerEntrypoint`; `internal/deployments/app_publish_test.go::TestAppPublisherCopiesListenerEntrypoint`; `internal/deployments/runtime_test.go::TestLoadManifestDefaultsListenerEnabled`. | `Proved` | Current Go still carries listener metadata and copied listener files inside published deployment snapshots even though session runtime startup is tracked separately below. |
| `DL-02` | The deployment listener runtime fails closed on a missing entrypoint or missing session id, keys listener handles by `(shareSessionId, projectConfigId)`, accepts either `build_listener` or `build_callbacks`, and drops stopped/completed per-session handles and loaded modules. | Source docs: `docs/reference/setta-listener-examples.md`; `docs/reference/sharing-mode-vs-ide-mode.md`; source retained runtime: `backend/setta/share/listener_runtime.py`; source tests: `tests/share/test_listener_runtime.py::{test_missing_entrypoint_raises,test_requires_session_id,test_uses_build_callbacks_fallback_when_build_listener_is_missing,test_stop_session_listener_and_stop_all,test_session_handles_are_scoped_by_config,test_completed_listener_task_drops_handle_and_module}`. | Current Go deployment runtime only manages overlays/sessions in `internal/deployments/runtime.go`; deployment HTTP bootstrap paths in `internal/httpapi/deployment_routes.go` create sessions but never launch a retained listener runtime; websocket wiring in `internal/wsapi/routes.go` still assumes a client-initiated listener websocket rather than a deployment-owned runtime manager. | `Missing` | `DP-08` separately tracks the missing auto-start/session-bootstrap behavior. This row captures the deeper retained-runtime lifecycle contract that still has no Go equivalent once a deployment listener is published. |
